<div class="thumb tright">
<div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px">
<div class="center" style="font-weight:bold">Selected battles</div><div class="thumbcaption" style="font-size:75%">
<a class="mw-kartographer-map mw-kartographer-container center" style="width: 250px; height: 100px;" data-mw-kartographer="mapframe" data-style="osm-intl" data-width="250" data-height="100" data-zoom="1" data-lat="36" data-lon="38" data-overlays="[&quot;_ccc35bd9aa95ee1c73eedab751e4e8cdd1f6c11b&quot;]" href="/wiki/Special:Map/1/36/38/en"><img src="https://maps.wikimedia.org/img/osm-intl,1,36,38,250x100.png?lang=en&amp;domain=en.wikipedia.org&amp;title=Arthur+Wellesley%2C+1st+Duke+of+Wellington&amp;revid=1175532567&amp;groups=_ccc35bd9aa95ee1c73eedab751e4e8cdd1f6c11b" width="250" height="100" decoding="async" srcset="https://maps.wikimedia.org/img/osm-intl,1,36,38,250x100@2x.png?lang=en&amp;domain=en.wikipedia.org&amp;title=Arthur+Wellesley%2C+1st+Duke+of+Wellington&amp;revid=1175532567&amp;groups=_ccc35bd9aa95ee1c73eedab751e4e8cdd1f6c11b 2x" alt="Map" /></a> </div>
<div class="thumbcaption" style="font-size:75%"><a class="mw-kartographer-maplink" data-mw-kartographer="maplink" data-style="osm-intl" href="/wiki/Special:Map/1/36/38/en" data-zoom="1" data-lat="36" data-lon="38" data-overlays="[&quot;_78d8a040c6241d9953b980891d5a140582f315da&quot;]">[Interactive fullscreen map + nearby articles]</a></div>
<div class="thumbcaption"><span style="margin:0px; font-size:90%;"><span style="border:none; background-color:olive; color:olive;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</span>&#160;1799–1803</span> <span style="margin:0px; font-size:90%;"><span style="border:none; background-color:navy; color:navy;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</span>&#160;1807–1813</span> <span style="margin:0px; font-size:90%;"><span style="border:none; background-color:maroon; color:maroon;">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</span>&#160;1815</span></div>
</div></div>
<p><b>Arthur Wellesley, 1st Duke of Wellington</b>, <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:small; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Knight_of_the_Order_of_the_Garter" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight of the Order of the Garter">KG</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Knight_Grand_Cross_of_the_Order_of_the_Bath" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight Grand Cross of the Order of the Bath">GCB</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Knight_Grand_Cross_of_the_Royal_Guelphic_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight Grand Cross of the Royal Guelphic Order">GCH</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">PC</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Fellow_of_the_Royal_Society" title="Fellow of the Royal Society">FRS</a></span> (<span title="Name at birth"><a href="/wiki/N%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Né">né</a>&#160;<b>Wesley</b></span>; 1 May 1769 – 14 September 1852) was an <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish_people" title="Anglo-Irish people">Anglo-Irish</a> statesman, soldier, and <a href="/wiki/British_Tory" class="mw-redirect" title="British Tory">British Tory</a> politician who was one of the leading military and political figures of <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland" title="United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland">19th-century Britain</a>, serving twice as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">prime minister of the United Kingdom</a>. He is among the commanders who won and ended the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a> when the <a href="/wiki/Seventh_Coalition" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh Coalition">Seventh Coalition</a> defeated <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleon</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Waterloo" title="Battle of Waterloo">Battle of Waterloo</a> in 1815.
</p><p>Wellesley was born in <a href="/wiki/Dublin" title="Dublin">Dublin</a> into the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Ascendancy" title="Protestant Ascendancy">Protestant Ascendancy</a> in <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ireland" title="Kingdom of Ireland">Ireland</a>. He was commissioned as an <a href="/wiki/Ensign_(rank)" title="Ensign (rank)">ensign</a> in the <a href="/wiki/British_Army" title="British Army">British Army</a> in 1787, serving in Ireland as <a href="/wiki/Aide-de-camp" title="Aide-de-camp">aide-de-camp</a> to two successive <a href="/wiki/Lord_Lieutenant_of_Ireland" title="Lord Lieutenant of Ireland">lords lieutenant of Ireland</a>. He was also elected as a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(United_Kingdom)" title="Member of Parliament (United Kingdom)">member of Parliament</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Irish_House_of_Commons" title="Irish House of Commons">Irish House of Commons</a>. He was a colonel by 1796 and saw <a href="/wiki/Low_Countries_theatre_of_the_War_of_the_First_Coalition" title="Low Countries theatre of the War of the First Coalition">action in the Netherlands</a> and in India, where he fought in the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Anglo-Mysore_War" title="Fourth Anglo-Mysore War">Fourth Anglo-Mysore War</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Seringapatam_(1799)" title="Siege of Seringapatam (1799)">Battle of Seringapatam</a>. He was appointed governor of <a href="/wiki/Seringapatam" class="mw-redirect" title="Seringapatam">Seringapatam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mysore" title="Mysore">Mysore</a> in 1799 and, as a newly appointed major-general, won a decisive victory over the <a href="/wiki/Maratha_Empire#Confederacy_era" title="Maratha Empire">Maratha Confederacy</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Assaye" title="Battle of Assaye">Battle of Assaye</a> in 1803.
</p><p>Wellesley rose to prominence as a general during the <a href="/wiki/Peninsular_War" title="Peninsular War">Peninsular campaign</a> of the Napoleonic Wars, and was promoted to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Field_marshal_(United_Kingdom)" title="Field marshal (United Kingdom)">field marshal</a> after leading the allied forces to victory against the <a href="/wiki/First_French_Empire" title="First French Empire">French Empire</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vitoria" title="Battle of Vitoria">Battle of Vitoria</a> in 1813. Following Napoleon's exile in 1814, he served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_the_United_Kingdom_to_France" title="List of ambassadors of the United Kingdom to France">ambassador to France</a> and was made <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington_(title)" title="Duke of Wellington (title)">Duke of Wellington</a>. During the <a href="/wiki/Hundred_Days" title="Hundred Days">Hundred Days</a> in 1815, he commanded the allied army which, together with a <a href="/wiki/Prussian_Army" title="Prussian Army">Prussian Army</a> under Field Marshal <a href="/wiki/Gebhard_Leberecht_von_Bl%C3%BCcher" title="Gebhard Leberecht von Blücher">Gebhard von Blücher</a>, defeated Napoleon at Waterloo. Wellington's <a href="/wiki/Military_career_of_Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington" title="Military career of Arthur Wellesley, 1st Duke of Wellington">battle record</a> is exemplary; he ultimately participated in some 60 battles during the course of his military career.
</p><p>Wellington is famous for his adaptive defensive style of warfare, resulting in several victories against numerically superior forces while minimising his own losses. He is regarded as one of the greatest defensive commanders of all time, and many of his tactics and battle plans are still studied in military academies around the world. After the end of his active military career, he returned to politics. He was twice British prime minister as a member of the Tory party from 1828 to 1830 and for a little less than a month in 1834. He oversaw the passage of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Relief_Act_1829" title="Roman Catholic Relief Act 1829">Roman Catholic Relief Act 1829</a>, while he opposed the <a href="/wiki/Reform_Act_1832" title="Reform Act 1832">Reform Act 1832</a>. He continued as one of the leading figures in the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Lords" title="House of Lords">House of Lords</a> until his retirement and remained <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-Chief_of_the_Forces" title="Commander-in-Chief of the Forces">Commander-in-Chief of the British Army</a> until <a href="/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_the_Duke_of_Wellington" title="Death and state funeral of the Duke of Wellington">his death</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Wellesley was born into an <a href="/wiki/Aristocratic" class="mw-redirect" title="Aristocratic">aristocratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglo-Irish">Anglo-Irish</a> family, belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Ascendancy" title="Protestant Ascendancy">Protestant Ascendancy</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ireland" title="Kingdom of Ireland">Ireland</a> as <a href="/wiki/The_Honourable" title="The Honourable">The Hon.</a> Arthur Wesley.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWellesley200816_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWellesley200816-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley was born the son of <a href="/wiki/Anne_Wellesley,_Countess_of_Mornington" title="Anne Wellesley, Countess of Mornington">Anne Wellesley, Countess of Mornington</a> and <a href="/wiki/Garret_Wesley,_1st_Earl_of_Mornington" title="Garret Wesley, 1st Earl of Mornington">Garret Wesley, 1st Earl of Mornington</a>. His father, Garret Wesley, was the son of <a href="/wiki/Richard_Wesley,_1st_Baron_Mornington" title="Richard Wesley, 1st Baron Mornington">Richard Wesley, 1st Baron Mornington</a> and had a short career in politics representing the constituency <a href="/wiki/Trim_(Parliament_of_Ireland_constituency)" title="Trim (Parliament of Ireland constituency)">Trim</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Irish_House_of_Commons" title="Irish House of Commons">Irish House of Commons</a> before succeeding his father as <a href="/wiki/Baron_Mornington" class="mw-redirect" title="Baron Mornington">2nd Baron Mornington</a> in 1758. Garret Wesley was also an accomplished <a href="/wiki/Composer" title="Composer">composer</a> and in recognition of his musical and philanthropic achievements was elevated to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Mornington" title="Earl of Mornington">Earl of Mornington</a> in 1760.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELongford197153_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELongford197153-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley's mother was the eldest daughter of <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Hill-Trevor,_1st_Viscount_Dungannon" title="Arthur Hill-Trevor, 1st Viscount Dungannon">Arthur Hill-Trevor, 1st Viscount Dungannon</a>, after whom Wellesley was named.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESevern200713_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESevern200713-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Wellesley was the sixth of nine children born to the Earl and Countess of Mornington. His siblings included <a href="/wiki/Richard_Wellesley,_1st_Marquess_Wellesley" title="Richard Wellesley, 1st Marquess Wellesley">Richard, Viscount Wellesley</a> (1760–1842); later 1st <a href="/wiki/Marquess_Wellesley" class="mw-redirect" title="Marquess Wellesley">Marquess Wellesley</a>, 2nd <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Mornington" title="Earl of Mornington">Earl of Mornington</a>, <a href="/wiki/Baron_Maryborough" class="mw-redirect" title="Baron Maryborough">Baron Maryborough</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_of_Parliament2013_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_of_Parliament2013-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Birth_date_and_place">Birth date and place</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Birth date and place">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The exact date and location of Wellesley's birth is not known; however, biographers mostly follow the same contemporary newspaper evidence which states that he was born on 1 May 1769, <sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> the day before he was baptised in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Church,_Aungier_Street,_Dublin" title="St. Peter&#39;s Church, Aungier Street, Dublin">St Peters Church, Dublin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArtware_Fine_Art2020_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArtware_Fine_Art2020-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuedalla1997480_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuedalla1997480-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> However, <a href="#CITEREFLloyd1899">Lloyd (1899)</a>, p.&#160;170 states "registry of St. Peter's Church, Dublin, shows that he was christened there on 30 April 1769". His baptismal font was donated to <a href="/wiki/St._Nahi%27s_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Nahi&#39;s Church">St. Nahi's Church</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dundrum,_Dublin" title="Dundrum, Dublin">Dundrum, Dublin</a>, in 1914. Wellesley was most likely born at his parents' townhouse, <a href="/wiki/Mornington_House" title="Mornington House">Mornington House</a> at 24 Upper <a href="/wiki/Merrion_Street" title="Merrion Street">Merrion Street</a>, Dublin, which now forms part of the <a href="/wiki/Merrion_Hotel" title="Merrion Hotel">Merrion Hotel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGash2011_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGash2011-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> This contrasts with reports that his mother Anne, Countess of Mornington, recalled in 1815 that he had been born at 6 Merrion Street, Dublin.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Other places have been put forward as the location of his birth, include the Dublin <a href="/wiki/Packet_boat" title="Packet boat">packet boat</a> and the Wellesley townhouse in Trim, County Meath.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Childhood">Childhood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Childhood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dangan_Castle,_Co_Meath,_Ireland,_1840.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg/220px-Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg/330px-Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg/440px-Dangan_Castle%2C_Co_Meath%2C_Ireland%2C_1840.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1226" data-file-height="886" /></a><figcaption>Wellesley spent much of his early childhood at his family's <a href="/wiki/Ancestral_home" title="Ancestral home">ancestral home</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dangan_Castle" title="Dangan Castle">Dangan Castle</a> in <a href="/wiki/County_Meath" title="County Meath">County Meath</a>, Ireland (engraving, 1842).</figcaption></figure>
<p>Wellesley spent most of his childhood at his family's two homes, the first a large house in Dublin, <a href="/wiki/Mornington_House" title="Mornington House">Mornington House</a>, and the second <a href="/wiki/Dangan_Castle" title="Dangan Castle">Dangan Castle</a>, 3 miles (5&#160;km) north of <a href="/wiki/Summerhill,_County_Meath" title="Summerhill, County Meath">Summerhill</a> in <a href="/wiki/County_Meath" title="County Meath">County Meath</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes20026–7_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes20026–7-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> In 1781, Arthur's father died and his eldest brother <a href="/wiki/Richard_Wellesley,_1st_Marquess_Wellesley" title="Richard Wellesley, 1st Marquess Wellesley">Richard</a> inherited his father's earldom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes20028_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes20028-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He went to the diocesan school in <a href="/wiki/Trim,_County_Meath" title="Trim, County Meath">Trim</a> when at Dangan, Mr Whyte's Academy when in Dublin, and Brown's School in Chelsea when in London. He then enrolled at <a href="/wiki/Eton_College" title="Eton College">Eton College</a>, where he studied from 1781 to 1784.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes20028_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes20028-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> His loneliness there caused him to hate it, and makes it highly unlikely that he actually said "The Battle of Waterloo was won on the playing fields of Eton", a quotation which is often attributed to him. Moreover, Eton had no playing fields at the time. In 1785, a lack of success at Eton, combined with a shortage of family funds due to his father's death, forced the young Wellesley and his mother to move to <a href="/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels">Brussels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes20029_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes20029-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Until his early twenties, Arthur showed little sign of distinction and his mother grew increasingly concerned at his idleness, stating, "I don't know what I shall do with my awkward son Arthur."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes20029_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes20029-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1786, Arthur enrolled in the French Royal Academy of Equitation in <a href="/wiki/Angers" title="Angers">Angers</a>, where he progressed significantly, becoming a good horseman and learning French, which later proved very useful.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200219–20_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200219–20-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Upon returning to England later the same year, he astonished his mother with his improvement.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200220_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200220-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_military_career">Early military career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Early military career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Military_career_of_Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington" title="Military career of Arthur Wellesley, 1st Duke of Wellington">Military career of Arthur Wellesley, 1st Duke of Wellington</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ireland">Ireland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Ireland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dcastlemaindoor.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Dcastlemaindoor.jpg/220px-Dcastlemaindoor.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Dcastlemaindoor.jpg/330px-Dcastlemaindoor.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Dcastlemaindoor.jpg/440px-Dcastlemaindoor.jpg 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="384" /></a><figcaption>Beginning in 1787, Wellesley served at <a href="/wiki/Dublin_Castle" title="Dublin Castle">Dublin Castle</a> (pictured) as <a href="/wiki/Aide-de-camp" title="Aide-de-camp">aide-de-camp</a> to two successive <a href="/wiki/Lord_Lieutenant_of_Ireland" title="Lord Lieutenant of Ireland">Lords Lieutenant of Ireland</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Despite his new promise, Wellesley had yet to find a job and his family was still short of money, so upon the advice of his mother, his brother Richard asked his friend the <a href="/wiki/Charles_Manners,_4th_Duke_of_Rutland" title="Charles Manners, 4th Duke of Rutland">Duke of Rutland</a> (then <a href="/wiki/Lord_Lieutenant_of_Ireland" title="Lord Lieutenant of Ireland">Lord Lieutenant of Ireland</a>) to consider Arthur for a commission in the Army.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200220_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200220-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Soon afterward, on 7 March 1787, he was gazetted <a href="/wiki/Ensign_(rank)" title="Ensign (rank)">ensign</a> in the <a href="/wiki/73rd_(Perthshire)_Regiment_of_Foot" title="73rd (Perthshire) Regiment of Foot">73rd Regiment of Foot</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> In October, with the assistance of his brother, he was assigned as <i><a href="/wiki/Aide-de-camp" title="Aide-de-camp">aide-de-camp</a></i>, on ten <a href="/wiki/Shilling" title="Shilling">shillings</a> a day (twice his pay as an ensign), to the new Lord Lieutenant of Ireland, <a href="/wiki/George_Nugent-Temple-Grenville,_1st_Marquess_of_Buckingham" title="George Nugent-Temple-Grenville, 1st Marquess of Buckingham">Lord Buckingham</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> He was also transferred to the new <a href="/wiki/76th_Regiment_of_Foot" title="76th Regiment of Foot">76th Regiment</a> forming in Ireland and on Christmas Day, 1787, was promoted <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_(British_Army_and_Royal_Marines)" title="Lieutenant (British Army and Royal Marines)">lieutenant</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200221-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> During his time in Dublin his duties were mainly social; attending balls, entertaining guests and providing advice to Buckingham. While in Ireland, he overextended himself in borrowing due to his occasional gambling, but in his defence stated that "I have often known what it was to be in want of money, but I have never got helplessly into debt".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200222_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200222-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 23 January 1788, he transferred into the <a href="/wiki/41st_(Welch)_Regiment_of_Foot" title="41st (Welch) Regiment of Foot">41st Regiment of Foot</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> then again on 25 June 1789 he transferred to the <a href="/wiki/12th_Royal_Lancers" title="12th Royal Lancers">12th (Prince of Wales's) Regiment of (Light) Dragoons</a><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and, according to military historian <a href="/wiki/Richard_Holmes_(military_historian)" title="Richard Holmes (military historian)">Richard Holmes</a>, he also reluctantly entered politics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200222_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200222-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Shortly before the general election of 1789, he went to the <a href="/wiki/Rotten_borough" class="mw-redirect" title="Rotten borough">rotten borough</a> of <a href="/wiki/Trim_(Parliament_of_Ireland_constituency)" title="Trim (Parliament of Ireland constituency)">Trim</a> to speak against the granting of the title "<a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_the_City" title="Freedom of the City">Freeman</a>" of Dublin to the parliamentary leader of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Patriot_Party" title="Irish Patriot Party">Irish Patriot Party</a>, <a href="/wiki/Henry_Grattan" title="Henry Grattan">Henry Grattan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200223_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200223-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Succeeding, he was later nominated and duly elected as a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(United_Kingdom)" title="Member of Parliament (United Kingdom)">Member of Parliament</a> (MP) for <a href="/wiki/Trim_(Parliament_of_Ireland_constituency)" title="Trim (Parliament of Ireland constituency)">Trim</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Irish_House_of_Commons" title="Irish House of Commons">Irish House of Commons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Because of the limited <a href="/wiki/Suffrage" title="Suffrage">suffrage</a> at the time, he sat in a parliament where at least two-thirds of the members owed their election to the landowners of fewer than a hundred boroughs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley continued to serve at <a href="/wiki/Dublin_Castle" title="Dublin Castle">Dublin Castle</a>, voting with the government in the Irish parliament over the next two years. He became a <a href="/wiki/Captain_(OF-2)" class="mw-redirect" title="Captain (OF-2)">captain</a> on 30 January 1791, and was transferred to the <a href="/wiki/58th_(Rutlandshire)_Regiment_of_Foot" title="58th (Rutlandshire) Regiment of Foot">58th Regiment of Foot</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200224-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuke_of_Wellington&#39;s_Regiment2006_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuke_of_Wellington&#39;s_Regiment2006-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 31 October, he transferred to the <a href="/wiki/18th_Royal_Hussars" title="18th Royal Hussars">18th Light Dragoons</a><sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and it was during this period that he grew increasingly attracted to <a href="/wiki/Catherine_Wellesley,_Duchess_of_Wellington" title="Catherine Wellesley, Duchess of Wellington">Kitty Pakenham</a>, the daughter of <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Longford" title="Earl of Longford">Edward Pakenham, 2nd Baron Longford</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200225_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200225-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> She was described as being full of 'gaiety and charm'.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENumber102011_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENumber102011-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> In 1793, he proposed, but was turned down by <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Pakenham,_2nd_Earl_of_Longford" title="Thomas Pakenham, 2nd Earl of Longford">her brother Thomas</a>, Earl of Longford, who considered Wellesley to be a young man, in debt, with very poor prospects.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200226_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200226-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> An aspiring amateur musician, Wellesley, devastated by the rejection, burnt his <a href="/wiki/Violin" title="Violin">violins</a> in anger, and resolved to pursue a military career in earnest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200227_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200227-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> He became a <a href="/wiki/Major" title="Major">major</a> <a href="/wiki/Sale_of_commissions" class="mw-redirect" title="Sale of commissions">by purchase</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington%27s_Regiment" title="Duke of Wellington&#39;s Regiment">33rd Regiment</a> in 1793.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200225_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200225-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> A few months later, in September, his brother lent him more money and with it he purchased a <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_colonel" title="Lieutenant colonel">lieutenant-colonelcy</a> in the 33rd.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200228_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200228-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Netherlands">Netherlands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Netherlands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Low_Countries_theatre_of_the_War_of_the_First_Coalition" title="Low Countries theatre of the War of the First Coalition">Flanders Campaign</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg/180px-Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="215" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg/270px-Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg/360px-Arthur_Wellesley_by_John_Hoppner.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2400" data-file-height="2867" /></a><figcaption>Wellesley as Lieutenant Colonel, aged c. 26, in the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington%27s_Regiment" title="Duke of Wellington&#39;s Regiment">33rd Regiment</a>. Portrait by <a href="/wiki/John_Hoppner" title="John Hoppner">John Hoppner</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1793, the Duke of York was sent to <a href="/wiki/Flanders" title="Flanders">Flanders</a> in command of the British contingent of an allied force destined for the invasion of France. In June 1794, Wellesley with the 33rd regiment set sail from <a href="/wiki/Cork_Harbour" title="Cork Harbour">Cork</a> bound for <a href="/wiki/Ostend" title="Ostend">Ostend</a> as part of an expedition bringing reinforcements for the army in Flanders. They arrived too late to participate, and joined the Duke of York as he was pulling back towards the Netherlands. On 15 September 1794, at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Boxtel" title="Battle of Boxtel">Battle of Boxtel</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200230_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200230-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> east of <a href="/wiki/Breda" title="Breda">Breda</a>, Wellington, in temporary command of his brigade, had his first experience of battle. During General <a href="/wiki/Ralph_Abercromby" title="Ralph Abercromby">Abercromby's</a> withdrawal in the face of superior French forces, the 33rd held off enemy cavalry, allowing neighbouring units to retreat safely. During the extremely harsh winter that followed, Wellesley and his regiment formed part of an allied force holding the defence line along the <a href="/wiki/Waal_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Waal River">Waal River</a>. The 33rd, along with the rest of the army, suffered heavy losses from attrition and illness. Wellesley's health was also affected by the damp environment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200231_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200231-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Though the campaign was to end disastrously, with the British army driven out of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">United Provinces</a> into the German states, Wellesley became more aware of battle tactics, including the use of lines of infantry against advancing columns, and the merits of supporting sea-power.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200230_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200230-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> He understood that the failure of the campaign was due in part to the faults of the leaders and the poor organisation at headquarters.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200232_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200232-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> He remarked later of his time in the Netherlands that "At least I learned what not to do, and that is always a valuable lesson".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200232_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200232-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Returning to England in March 1795, he was reinstated as a member of parliament for Trim.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> He hoped to be given the position of secretary of war in the new Irish government but the new lord-lieutenant, <a href="/wiki/John_Pratt,_1st_Marquess_Camden" title="John Pratt, 1st Marquess Camden">Lord Camden</a>, was only able to offer him the post of <a href="/wiki/Surveyor-General_of_the_Ordnance_(Ireland)" class="mw-redirect" title="Surveyor-General of the Ordnance (Ireland)">Surveyor-General of the Ordnance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Declining the post, he returned to his regiment, now at <a href="/wiki/Southampton" title="Southampton">Southampton</a> preparing to set sail for the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies" title="West Indies">West Indies</a>. After seven weeks at sea, a storm forced the fleet back to <a href="/wiki/Poole" title="Poole">Poole</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200233-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The 33rd was given time to recuperate and a few months later, <a href="/wiki/Whitehall" title="Whitehall">Whitehall</a> decided to send the regiment to India. Wellesley was promoted full <a href="/wiki/Colonel" title="Colonel">colonel</a> by seniority on 3 May 1796<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> and a few weeks later set sail for <a href="/wiki/Calcutta" class="mw-redirect" title="Calcutta">Calcutta</a> with his regiment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200234_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200234-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="India">India</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: India">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Arriving in <a href="/wiki/Calcutta" class="mw-redirect" title="Calcutta">Calcutta</a> in February 1797 he spent 5 months there, before being sent in August to a brief expedition to the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>, where he established a list of new hygiene precautions for his men to deal with the unfamiliar climate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200240_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200240-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Returning in November to <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>, he learnt that his elder brother <a href="/wiki/Richard_Wellesley,_1st_Marquess_Wellesley" title="Richard Wellesley, 1st Marquess Wellesley">Richard</a>, now known as Lord Mornington, had been appointed as the new <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_India" title="Governor-General of India">Governor-General of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200241_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200241-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1798, he changed the spelling of his surname to "Wellesley"; up to this time he was still known as Wesley, which his eldest brother considered the ancient and proper spelling.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200241_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200241-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELongford197154_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELongford197154-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Fourth_Anglo-Mysore_War">Fourth Anglo-Mysore War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Fourth Anglo-Mysore War">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Anglo-Mysore_War" title="Fourth Anglo-Mysore War">Fourth Anglo-Mysore War</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg/220px-Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="254" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg/330px-Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg/440px-Arthur_Wellesley%2C_1st_Duke_of_Wellington_by_Robert_Home.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2400" data-file-height="2766" /></a><figcaption>Wellesley in India, wearing his major-general's uniform. Portrait by <a href="/wiki/Robert_Home" title="Robert Home">Robert Home</a>, 1804.</figcaption></figure>
<p>As part of the campaign to extend the rule of the <a href="/wiki/British_East_India_Company" class="mw-redirect" title="British East India Company">British East India Company</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Anglo-Mysore_War" title="Fourth Anglo-Mysore War">Fourth Anglo-Mysore War</a> broke out in 1798 against the Sultan of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Mysore" title="Kingdom of Mysore">Mysore</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tipu_Sultan" title="Tipu Sultan">Tipu Sultan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200242_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200242-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Arthur's brother Richard ordered that an armed force be sent to capture <a href="/wiki/Seringapatam" class="mw-redirect" title="Seringapatam">Seringapatam</a> and defeat Tipu. During the war, rockets were used on several occasions. Wellesley was almost defeated by Tipu's Diwan, <a href="/wiki/Purnaiah" title="Purnaiah">Purnaiah</a>, at the Battle of Sultanpet Tope. Quoting Forrest,
</p>
<blockquote><p>At this point (near the village of Sultanpet, Figure 5) there was a large tope, or grove, which gave shelter to Tipu's rocketmen and had obviously to be cleaned out before the siege could be pressed closer to Srirangapattana island. The commander chosen for this operation was Col. Wellesley, but advancing towards the tope after dark on the 5th April 1799, he was set upon with rockets and musket-fires, lost his way and, as Beatson politely puts it, had to "postpone the attack" until a more favourable opportunity should offer.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEForrest1970283_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEForrest1970283-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The following day, Wellesley launched a fresh attack with a larger force, and took the whole position without any killed in action.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> On 22 April 1799, twelve days before the main battle, rocketeers maneuvered to the rear of the British encampment, then 'threw a great number of rockets at the same instant' to signal the beginning of an assault by 6,000 Indian infantry and a corps of Frenchmen, all ordered by Mir Golam Hussain and Mohomed Hulleen Mir Miran. The rockets had a range of about 1,000 yards. Some burst in the air like <a href="/wiki/Shell_(projectile)" title="Shell (projectile)">shells</a>. Others, called ground rockets, would rise again on striking the ground and bound along in a serpentine motion until their force was spent. According to one British observer, a young English officer named Bayly: "So pestered were we with the rocket boys that there was no moving without danger from the destructive missiles ...". He continued:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<blockquote><p>The rockets and musketry from 20,000 of the enemy were incessant. No hail could be thicker. Every illumination of blue lights was accompanied by a shower of rockets, some of which entered the head of the column, passing through to the rear, causing death, wounds, and dreadful lacerations from the long bamboos of twenty or thirty feet, which are invariably attached to them.</p></blockquote>
<p>Under the command of <a href="/wiki/George_Harris,_1st_Baron_Harris" title="George Harris, 1st Baron Harris">General Harris</a>, some 24,000 troops were dispatched to <a href="/wiki/Madras" class="mw-redirect" title="Madras">Madras</a> (to join an equal force being sent from <a href="/wiki/Bombay" class="mw-redirect" title="Bombay">Bombay</a> in the west).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200249_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200249-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Arthur and the 33rd sailed to join them in August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200244_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200244-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After extensive and careful logistic preparation (which would become one of Wellesley's main attributes)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200247_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200247-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> the 33rd left with the main force in December and travelled across 250 miles (402&#160;km) of jungle from Madras to Mysore.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200247_50-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200247-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> On account of his brother, during the journey, Wellesley was given an additional command, that of chief advisor to the <a href="/wiki/Nizam_Ali_Khan,_Asaf_Jah_II" title="Nizam Ali Khan, Asaf Jah II">Nizam</a> of <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad_State" title="Hyderabad State">Hyderabad</a>'s army (sent to accompany the British force).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200249_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200249-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> This position was to cause friction among many of the senior officers (some of whom were senior to Wellesley).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200251_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200251-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Much of this friction was put to rest after the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Mallavelly" title="Battle of Mallavelly">Battle of Mallavelly</a>, some 20 miles (32&#160;km) from Seringapatam, in which Harris' army attacked a large part of the sultan's army. During the battle, Wellesley led his men, in a line of battle of two ranks, against the enemy to a gentle ridge and gave the order to fire.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200253_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200253-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> After an extensive repetition of volleys, followed by a bayonet charge, the 33rd, in conjunction with the rest of Harris's force, forced Tipu's infantry to retreat.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200263_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200263-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Seringapatam">Seringapatam</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Seringapatam">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Immediately after their arrival at <a href="/wiki/Srirangapatna" title="Srirangapatna">Seringapatam</a> on 5 April 1799, the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Seringapatam_(1799)" title="Siege of Seringapatam (1799)">Battle of Seringapatam</a> began and Wellesley was ordered to lead a night attack on the village of Sultanpettah, adjacent to the fortress to clear the way for the artillery.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200256_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200256-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Because of a variety of factors including the Mysorean army's strong defensive preparations and the darkness the attack failed with 25 casualties due to confusion among the British. Wellesley suffered a minor injury to his knee from a spent musket-ball.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200257_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200257-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMacquarie_University2008_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacquarie_University2008-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>  Although they would re-attack successfully the next day, after time to scout ahead the enemy's positions, the affair affected Wellesley. He resolved "never to attack an enemy who is preparing and strongly posted, and whose posts have not been reconnoitred by daylight".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258_47-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
<a href="/wiki/Lewin_Bentham_Bowring" title="Lewin Bentham Bowring">Lewin Bentham Bowring</a> gives this alternative account:
</p>
<blockquote><p>One of these groves, called the Sultanpet Tope, was intersected by deep ditches, watered from a channel running in an easterly direction about a mile from the fort. <a href="/wiki/Sir_David_Baird,_1st_Baronet" title="Sir David Baird, 1st Baronet">General Baird</a> was directed to scour this grove and dislodge the enemy, but on his advancing with this object on the night of the 5th, he found the tope unoccupied. The next day, however, the Mysore troops again took possession of the ground, and as it was absolutely necessary to expel them, two columns were detached at sunset for the purpose. The first of these, under Colonel Shawe, got possession of a ruined village, which it successfully held. The second column, under Colonel Wellesley, on advancing into the tope, was at once attacked in the darkness of night by a tremendous fire of musketry and rockets. The men, floundering about amidst the trees and the water-courses, at last broke, and fell back in disorder, some being killed and a few taken prisoners. In the confusion Colonel Wellesley was himself struck on the knee by a spent ball, and narrowly escaped falling into the hands of the enemy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBowring189384–85_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowring189384–85-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>A few weeks later, after extensive artillery bombardment, a breach was opened in the main walls of the fortress of Seringapatam.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258_47-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200258-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> An attack led by <a href="/wiki/David_Baird,_1st_Baronet" class="mw-redirect" title="David Baird, 1st Baronet">Major-General Baird</a> secured the fortress. Wellesley secured the rear of the advance, posting guards at the breach and then stationed his regiment at the main palace.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200259_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200259-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> After hearing news of the death of the Tipu Sultan, Wellesley was the first at the scene to confirm his death, checking his pulse.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200260_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200260-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Over the coming day, Wellesley grew increasingly concerned over the lack of discipline among his men, who drank and pillaged the fortress and city. To restore order, several soldiers were <a href="/wiki/Flagellation" title="Flagellation">flogged</a> and four <a href="/wiki/Hanging" title="Hanging">hanged</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200262_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200262-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After battle and the resulting end of the war, the main force under General Harris left Seringapatam and Wellesley, aged 30, stayed behind to command the area as the new Governor of Seringapatam and Mysore. While in India, Wellesley was ill for a considerable time, first with severe <a href="/wiki/Diarrhoea" class="mw-redirect" title="Diarrhoea">diarrhoea</a> from the water and then with fever, followed by a serious skin infection caused by <a href="/wiki/Trichophyton" title="Trichophyton">trichophyton</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200267_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200267-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Wellesley was in charge of raising an Anglo-Indian expeditionary force in <a href="/wiki/Trincomalee" title="Trincomalee">Trincomali</a> in early 1801<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJerdanWorkmanMorleyArnold1832_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJerdanWorkmanMorleyArnold1832-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> for the capture of Batavia and Mauritius from the French. However, on the eve of its departure, orders arrived from England that it was to be sent to Egypt to co-operate with Sir Ralph Abercromby in the expulsion of the <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">French</a> from <a href="/wiki/Egypt" title="Egypt">Egypt</a>. Wellesley had been appointed second in command to Baird, but owing to ill health did not accompany the expedition on 9 April 1801. This was fortunate for Wellesley, since the vessel on which he was to have sailed sank in the Red Sea.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIntelligence_Branch19118–20_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIntelligence_Branch19118–20-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was promoted to <a href="/wiki/Brigadier_general_(United_Kingdom)" class="mw-redirect" title="Brigadier general (United Kingdom)">brigadier-general</a> on 17 July 1801. He took residence within the Sultan's summer palace and reformed the tax and justice systems in his province to maintain order and prevent bribery.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200263_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200263-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dhoondiah_Waugh_insurgency">Dhoondiah Waugh insurgency</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Dhoondiah Waugh insurgency">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1800, whilst serving as Governor of Mysore, Wellesley was tasked with putting down an <a href="/wiki/Insurgency" title="Insurgency">insurgency</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Dhoondiah_Waugh" class="mw-redirect" title="Dhoondiah Waugh">Dhoondiah Waugh</a>, formerly a <a href="/wiki/Patan_district" title="Patan district">Patan</a> trooper for <a href="/wiki/Tipu_Sultan" title="Tipu Sultan">Tipu Sultan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199388_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199388-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Having escaped after the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Seringapatam_(1799)" title="Siege of Seringapatam (1799)">fall of Seringapatam</a> he became a powerful brigand, raiding villages along the Maratha–Mysore border region.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200264_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200264-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite initial setbacks, the <a href="/wiki/East_India_Company" title="East India Company">East India Company</a> having pursued and destroyed his forces once already, forcing him into retreat in August 1799, he raised a sizeable force composed of disbanded Mysore soldiers, captured small outposts and forts in Mysore, and was receiving the support of several Maratha <i><a href="/wiki/Killedar" class="mw-redirect" title="Killedar">killedars</a></i> opposed to British occupation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201222_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201222-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> This drew the attention of the British administration, who were beginning to recognise him as more than just a bandit, as his raids, expansion and threats to destabilise British authority suddenly increased in 1800.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199394–96_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199394–96-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>  The death of Tipu Sultan had created a <a href="/wiki/Power_vacuum" title="Power vacuum">power vacuum</a> and Waugh was seeking to fill it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201222_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201222-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Given independent command of a combined East India Company and British Army force,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199397_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199397-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>  Wellesley ventured north to confront Waugh in June 1800, with an army of 8,000 infantry and cavalry, having learnt that Waugh's forces numbered over 50,000, although the majority (around 30,000) were <a href="/wiki/Irregular_military" title="Irregular military">irregular</a> light cavalry and unlikely to pose a serious threat to British infantry and artillery.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201233,_38_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201233,_38-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Throughout June–August 1800, Wellesley advanced through Waugh's territory, his troops <a href="/wiki/Escalade" title="Escalade">escalading</a> forts in turn and capturing each one with "trifling loss".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWellesley1852181,_190_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWellesley1852181,_190-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The forts generally offered little resistance due to their poor construction and design.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199397_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199397-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley did not have sufficient troops to garrison each fort and had to clear the surrounding area of insurgents before advancing to the next fort.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201234_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201234-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> On 31 July, he had "taken and destroyed Dhoondiah's baggage and six guns, and driven into the Malpoorba (where they were drowned) about five thousand people".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWellesley1852191_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWellesley1852191-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Dhoondiah continued to retreat, but his forces were rapidly deserting, he had no infantry and due to the <a href="/wiki/Monsoon" title="Monsoon">monsoon</a> weather flooding river crossings he could no longer outpace the British advance.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199399_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199399-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> On 10 September, at the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Battle_of_Conaghul&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Battle of Conaghul (page does not exist)">Battle of Conaghul</a>, Wellesley personally led a charge of 1,400 British dragoons and Indian cavalry, in single line with no reserve, against Dhoondiah and his remaining 5,000 cavalry.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWeller199399_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeller199399-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Dhoondiah was killed during the clash, his body was discovered and taken to the British camp tied to a cannon. With this victory, Wellesley's campaign was concluded, and British authority had been restored.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201237–38_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201237–38-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley then paid for the future upkeep of Dhoondiah's orphaned son.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200265_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200265-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Second_Anglo-Maratha_War">Second Anglo-Maratha War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Second Anglo-Maratha War">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Second_Anglo-Maratha_War" title="Second Anglo-Maratha War">Second Anglo-Maratha War</a></div>
<p>In September 1802, Wellesley learnt that he had been promoted to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Major-General_(United_Kingdom)" class="mw-redirect" title="Major-General (United Kingdom)">major-general</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> He had been gazetted on 29 April 1802, but the news took several months to reach him by sea. He remained at Mysore until November when he was sent to command an army in the <a href="/wiki/Second_Anglo-Maratha_War" title="Second Anglo-Maratha War">Second Anglo-Maratha War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When he determined that a long defensive war would ruin his army, Wellesley decided to act boldly to defeat the numerically larger force of the <a href="/wiki/Maratha_Empire" title="Maratha Empire">Maratha Empire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200273_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200273-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> With the logistic assembly of his army complete (24,000 men in total) he gave the order to break camp and attack the nearest Maratha fort on 8 August 1803.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269,_73_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200269,_73-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The fort surrendered on 12 August after an infantry attack had exploited an artillery-made breach in the wall. With the fort now in British control Wellesley was able to extend control southwards to the river <a href="/wiki/Godavari" class="mw-redirect" title="Godavari">Godavari</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200274_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200274-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Assaye.2C_Argaum_and_Gawilghur"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Assaye,_Argaum_and_Gawilghur">Assaye, Argaum and Gawilghur</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Assaye, Argaum and Gawilghur">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg/220px-Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg/330px-Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg/440px-Battle_of_Assaye.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="716" /></a><figcaption>Arthur Wellesley (mounted) at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Assaye" title="Battle of Assaye">Battle of Assaye</a> (engraving after <a href="/wiki/William_Heath_(artist)" title="William Heath (artist)">William Heath)</a>. Wellesley later remarked that it was his greatest victory.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Splitting his army into two forces to pursue and locate the main Marathas army (the second force, commanded by Colonel Stevenson was far smaller), Wellesley was preparing to rejoin his forces on 24 September. His intelligence, however, reported the location of the Marathas' main army, between two rivers near <a href="/wiki/Assaye" title="Assaye">Assaye</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200275_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200275-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> If he waited for the arrival of his second force, the Marathas would be able to mount a retreat, so Wellesley decided to launch an attack immediately.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200275_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200275-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 23 September, Wellesley led his forces over a ford in the river Kaitna and the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Assaye" title="Battle of Assaye">Battle of Assaye</a> commenced.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200277_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200277-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> After crossing the ford the infantry was reorganised into several lines and advanced against the Maratha infantry. Wellesley ordered his <a href="/wiki/Cavalry" title="Cavalry">cavalry</a> to exploit the flank of the Maratha army just near the village.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200277_82-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200277-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> During the battle Wellesley himself came under fire; two of his horses were shot from under him and he had to mount a third.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200280_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200280-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> At a crucial moment, Wellesley regrouped his forces and ordered Colonel Maxwell (later killed in the attack) to attack the eastern end of the Maratha position while Wellesley himself directed a renewed infantry attack against the centre.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200280_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200280-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An officer in the attack wrote of the importance of Wellesley's personal leadership: "The General was in the thick of the action the whole time&#160;... I never saw a man so cool and collected as he was&#160;... though I can assure you, till our troops got the order to advance the fate of the day seemed doubtful&#160;..."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELongford197193_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELongford197193-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> With some 6,000 Marathas killed or wounded, the enemy was routed, though Wellesley's force was in no condition to pursue. British casualties were heavy: the British losses amounted to 428 killed, 1,138 wounded and 18 missing (the British casualty figures were taken from Wellesley's own despatch).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMillar200682_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillar200682-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Wellesley was troubled by the loss of men and remarked that he hoped "I should not like to see again such loss as I sustained on 23 September, even if attended by such gain".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Years later, however, he remarked that Assaye and not Waterloo was the best battle he ever fought.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281_80-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200281-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite the damage done to the Maratha army, the battle did not end the war.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200282_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200282-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> A few months later in November, Wellesley attacked a larger force near <a href="/wiki/Argaon" title="Argaon">Argaum</a>, leading his army to victory again, with an astonishing 5,000 enemy dead at the cost of only 361 British casualties.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200282_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200282-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> A further successful attack at the fortress at <a href="/wiki/Gawilghur" title="Gawilghur">Gawilghur</a>, combined with the victory of <a href="/wiki/Gerard_Lake,_1st_Viscount_Lake" title="Gerard Lake, 1st Viscount Lake">General Lake</a> at <a href="/wiki/Delhi" title="Delhi">Delhi</a>, forced the Maratha to sign a peace settlement at <a href="/wiki/Anjangaon" title="Anjangaon">Anjangaon</a> (not concluded until a year later) called the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Surji-Anjangaon" title="Treaty of Surji-Anjangaon">Treaty of Surji-Anjangaon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200283_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200283-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Military historian Richard Holmes remarked that Wellesley's experiences in India had an important influence on his personality and military tactics, teaching him much about military matters that would prove vital to his success in the <a href="/wiki/Peninsular_War" title="Peninsular War">Peninsular War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200288_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200288-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> These included a strong sense of discipline through drill and order,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200287_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200287-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> the use of diplomacy to gain allies, and the vital necessity of a secure supply line. He also established high regard for the acquisition of intelligence through scouts and spies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200287_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200287-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> His personal tastes also developed, including dressing himself in white trousers, a dark tunic, with <a href="/wiki/Hessian_boot" class="mw-redirect" title="Hessian boot">Hessian boots</a> and black cocked hat (that later became synonymous as his style).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200286_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200286-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Leaving_India">Leaving India</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Leaving India">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg/220px-Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg/330px-Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg/440px-Major-General_the_Hon._Arthur_Wellesley_being_received_in_durbar_at_the_Chepauk_Palace_Madras_by_Azim_al-Daula_Nawab_of_the_Carnatic_18th_February_1805.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3918" data-file-height="2749" /></a><figcaption>Major-General Wellesley meeting with <a href="/wiki/Nawab" title="Nawab">Nawab</a> <a href="/wiki/Azim-ud-Daula" title="Azim-ud-Daula">Azim-ud-Daula</a>, 1805</figcaption></figure>
<p>Wellesley had grown tired of his time in India, remarking "I have served as long in India as any man ought who can serve anywhere else".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In June 1804 he applied for permission to return home and as a reward for his service in India he was made a <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Bath" title="Order of the Bath">Knight of the Bath</a> in September.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> While in India, Wellesley had amassed a fortune of £42,000 (considerable at the time), consisting mainly of <a href="/wiki/Prize_money" title="Prize money">prize money</a> from his campaign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284_91-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200284-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> When his brother's term as Governor-General of India ended in March 1805, the brothers returned together to England on <a href="/wiki/HMS_Howe_(1805)" title="HMS Howe (1805)">HMS <i>Howe</i></a>. Wellesley, coincidentally, stopped on his voyage at the island of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Helena" title="Saint Helena">Saint Helena</a> and stayed in the <a href="/wiki/Briars,_St_Helena" class="mw-redirect" title="Briars, St Helena">same building</a> in which <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon I">Napoleon I</a> would live during his later exile.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200285_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200285-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_to_Britain">Return to Britain</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Return to Britain">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Meeting_Nelson">Meeting Nelson</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Meeting Nelson">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In September 1805, Major-General Wellesley was newly returned from his campaigns in India and was not yet particularly well known to the public. He reported to the office of the <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_War_and_the_Colonies" title="Secretary of State for War and the Colonies">Secretary of State for War and the Colonies</a> to request a new assignment. In the waiting room, he met Vice-Admiral <a href="/wiki/Horatio_Nelson" class="mw-redirect" title="Horatio Nelson">Horatio Nelson</a>, already a known figure after his victories at the Nile and Copenhagen, who was briefly in England after months pursuing the French Toulon fleet to the West Indies and back. Some 30 years later, Wellington recalled a conversation that Nelson began with him which Wellesley found "almost all on his side in a style so vain and silly as to surprise and almost disgust me".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200292_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200292-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Nelson left the room to inquire who the young general was and, on his return, switched to a very different tone, discussing the war, the state of the colonies, and the geopolitical situation as between equals.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAustralian_Broadcasting_Corporation2005_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAustralian_Broadcasting_Corporation2005-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> On this second discussion, Wellington recalled, "I don't know that I ever had a conversation that interested me more".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELambert2005283_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELambert2005283-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> This was the only time that the two men met; Nelson was killed at his victory at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Trafalgar" title="Battle of Trafalgar">Trafalgar</a> seven weeks later.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes200292_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes200292-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Wellesley then served in the abortive <a href="/wiki/Hanover_Expedition" title="Hanover Expedition">Anglo-Russian expedition</a> to north Germany in 1805, taking a brigade to <a href="/wiki/Elbe" title="Elbe">Elbe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoberts2003axxiii_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts2003axxiii-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He then took a period of extended leave from the army and was elected as a <a href="/wiki/Tories_(political_faction)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tories (political faction)">Tory</a> member of the British parliament for <a href="/wiki/Rye,_East_Sussex" title="Rye, East Sussex">Rye</a> in January 1806.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeillands200338_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeillands200338-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> A year later, he was elected MP for <a href="/wiki/Newport_(Isle_of_Wight)_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Newport (Isle of Wight) (UK Parliament constituency)">Newport</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Isle_of_Wight" title="Isle of Wight">Isle of Wight</a>, and was then appointed to serve as <a href="/wiki/Chief_Secretary_for_Ireland" title="Chief Secretary for Ireland">Chief Secretary for Ireland</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Charles_Lennox,_4th_Duke_of_Richmond" title="Charles Lennox, 4th Duke of Richmond">Duke of Richmond</a>. At the same time, he was made a <a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">privy counsellor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeillands200338_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeillands200338-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> While in Ireland, he gave a verbal promise that the remaining <a href="/wiki/Penal_Laws_against_Irish_Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Penal Laws against Irish Catholics">Penal Laws</a> would be enforced with great moderation, perhaps an indication of his later willingness to support <a href="/wiki/Catholic_emancipation" title="Catholic emancipation">Catholic emancipation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELongford1971174_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELongford1971174-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="War_against_Denmark-Norway">War against Denmark-Norway</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: War against Denmark-Norway">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Copenhagen_(1807)" title="Battle of Copenhagen (1807)">Battle of Copenhagen (1807)</a></div>
<p>Wellesley was in Ireland in May 1807 when he heard of the British expedition to <a href="/wiki/Denmark-Norway" class="mw-redirect" title="Denmark-Norway">Denmark-Norway</a>. He decided to go, while maintaining his political appointments, and was appointed to command an infantry brigade in the <a href="/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Copenhagen" class="mw-redirect" title="Second Battle of Copenhagen">Second Battle of Copenhagen</a>, which took place in August. He fought at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_K%C3%B8ge" title="Battle of Køge">Køge</a>, during which the men under his command took 1,500 prisoners, with Wellesley later present during the surrender.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeillands200338_97-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeillands200338-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By 30 September, he had returned to England and was raised to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_general" title="Lieutenant general">lieutenant general</a> on 25 April 1808.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeillands200338_97-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeillands200338-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> In June 1808 he accepted the command of an expedition of 9,000 men. Preparing to sail for an attack on the Spanish colonies in <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a> (to assist the Latin American patriot <a href="/wiki/Francisco_de_Miranda" title="Francisco de Miranda">Francisco de Miranda</a>) his force was instead ordered to sail for <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, to take part in the Peninsular Campaign and rendezvous with 5,000 troops from <a href="/wiki/Gibraltar" title="Gibraltar">Gibraltar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeillands200339_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeillands200339-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHolmes2002102–103_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes2002102–103-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Peninsular_War">Peninsular War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arthur_Wellesley,_1st_Duke_of_Wellington&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Peninsular War">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Peninsular_War" title="Peninsular War">Peninsular War</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/British_Army_during_the_Napoleonic_Wars" title="British Army during the Napoleonic Wars">British Army during the Napoleonic Wars</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Portuguese_Army" title="Anglo-Portuguese Army">Anglo-Portuguese Army</a></div>
